Soldiers don't have to be actively defending the town against attacks to be considered defend soldiers. In other words, the defend-soldier-count is calculated by subtracting the attack-soldier-count from the total soldier-count.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if the computer player has at least 15 defend soldiers.*
```
(defrule
	(defend-soldier-count >= 15)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`attack-soldier-count`, `attack-warboat-count`, `defend-warboat-count`, `military-population`, `soldier-count`, `warboat-count`

相关 SN：`sn-consecutive-idle-unit-limit`, `sn-disable-attack-groups`, `sn-disable-defend-groups`, `sn-gather-defense-units`, `sn-gather-idle-soldiers-at-center`, `sn-maximum-town-size`, `sn-number-attack-groups`, `sn-percent-attack-soldiers`, `sn-safe-town-size`, `sn-task-ungrouped-soldiers`

### `defend-warboat-count`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Counting, Defense, Units · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the computer player's defend warboat count.**

Checks the computer player's defend warboat count. A defend warboat is a boat capable of attacking that is not assigned to boat attack groups. This includes warboats attacking with attack-now or with sn-number-boat-attack-groups set > 0. Warboats don't have to be actively defending against enemy warship attacks to be considered defend warboats. In other words, the defend-warboat-count is calculated by subtracting the attack-warboat-count from the total warboat-count.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if the computer player has at least 5 defend warboats.*
```
(defrule
	(defend-warboat-count >= 5)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`attack-soldier-count`, `attack-warboat-count`, `defend-soldier-count`, `military-population`, `soldier-count`, `warboat-count`

相关 SN：`sn-consecutive-idle-unit-limit`, `sn-disable-attack-groups`, `sn-number-attack-groups`, `sn-percent-attack-boats`

### `difficulty`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Game Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the difficulty setting.**

Checks the difficulty setting.

The ordering of difficulty settings is the opposite of what one would expect! Make sure that this is taken in account when using facts to compare difficulties. easiest > easy > moderate > hard > hardest (ie; treat easiest as a difficulty value of 4, easy as 3, moderate as 2, hard as 1, hardest as 0, and extreme as -1).

For testing certain difficulty levels see the code examples. It is counter intuitive!
(difficulty == easiest)True if the difficulty is easiest(difficulty > easiest)WRONG: This will never be true, Easiest is the "highest" number!(difficulty < hardest)WRONG: This will never be true, Hardest is the "lowest" number (Extreme is the lowest number in DE)!(difficulty <= moderate)This is true if the difficulty is Moderate, Hard, Hardest, or Extreme.(difficulty >= easy)This is true if the difficulty is Easy or Easiest.(difficulty > hard)Counter-intuitive - avoid (you probably want the opposite in fact, see below), this is true if the difficulty is Moderate, Easy or Easiest.(difficulty <= hard)This is true if the difficulty is Hard, Hardest, or Extreme.(difficulty > hardest)This is true if the difficulty is Hard, Moderate, Easy or Easiest

Because of the counter-intuitive ordering of difficulties, you may find it helpful to use [#load-if-defined](/commands/commands-details.html##load-if-defined) or [#load-if-not-defined](/commands/commands-details.html##load-if-not-defined) to check difficulty settings instead, such as #load-if-defined DIFFICULTY-HARD or #load-if-not-defined DIFFICULTY-HARDEST.
Full information on difficulty affecting aspects

Remember that easy is referred to as Standard in the game. This information about difficulty is from the CPSB about the hardcoded changes. Automatic changes to some sn values can be stopped with [sn-do-not-scale-for-difficulty-level](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-do-not-scale-for-difficulty-level); see this SN for more information.
Distance an enemy unit must be within when the computer player unit looks for a new target:easiest: LOS (can be modified by [sn-easiest-reaction-percentage](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-easiest-reaction-percentage))easy: LOS (can be modified by [sn-easier-reaction-percentage](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-easier-reaction-percentage))moderate: LOS * 2hard: LOS * 2hardest: LOS * 2Computer players ignore relics on the easiest level.Computer players do not attack villagers on the easiest and easy difficulty levels.If a non-exploring computer unit gets attacked, the computer player's attack delay for attack-group settings is modified:easiest: allow attacking one minute earliereasy: allow attacking two minutes earliermoderate: allow attacking immediatelyhard: allow attacking immediatelyhardest: allow attacking immediatelyAfter a wolf kills a unit, have it gorge itself (not attack again) for:easiest: 35 secondseasy: 30 secondsmoderate: 25 secondshard: 20 secondshardest: 15 secondsDistance a unit must be within when a wolf looks for a new target (UP only):DE removed the reaction distance modifier for predator animals (like Wolves, Snow Leopards) depending on difficulty and made it so that predator animals always find villagers within 6 tiles and other units within 4 tiles. Easiest difficulty on scenarios and campaigns will still use 4 tiles.easiest: LOS * 0.5easy: LOS * 0.75moderate: LOS * 2hard: LOS * 2hardest: LOS * 2Unit build (using villager for example) and research time (including age advancement):easiest: 200% (0:25 to 0:50)easy/standard: 133% (0:25 to 0:33)moderate: 100% (for DE it's 114% (0:25 to 0:28))hard: 100% (for DE it's 105% (0:25 to 0:26))hardest: 100%

Building construction appears to be unaffected.

For non-DE game versions, Hardest difficulty adds a hardcoded 500 of each resource at the beginning of the game and on reaching each new age. This cannot be disabled, but you can remove these resources with a negative [cc-add-resource](/commands/commands-details.html#cc-add-resource) or [up-cc-add-resource](/commands/commands-details.html#up-cc-add-resource) command. Also note that starting the game in later ages adds these bonuses incrementally (so up to 2000 for starting in the Imperial Age or Post-Imperial Age).

Each difficulty level will change certain SN values automatically (including when set manually) unless sn-do-not-scale-for-difficulty-level is set to 1. See [sn-do-not-scale-for-difficulty-level](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-do-not-scale-for-difficulty-level) for these values. Small additional note is that Hard also still makes SN changes, so it is recommended for a non-cheating AI to use sn-do-not-scale-for-difficulty-level so it can perform well on Hard.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Difficulty`

*示例：Checks if the difficulty is hard, hardest, or extreme.*
```
(defrule
	(difficulty <= hard)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关 SN：`sn-do-not-scale-for-difficulty-level`, `sn-easiest-reaction-percentage`, `sn-easier-reaction-percentage`

### `doctrine`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Goals · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks what the current doctrine is, similar to checking the value of a goal.**

Checks what the current doctrine is, similar to checking the value of a goal. The doctrine is always an integer value which is set with the [set-doctrine](/commands/commands-details.html#set-doctrine) command, and the doctrine command simply checks if the doctrine is currently equal to the given value. Unlike goals, there is only one doctrine that you can set, and you can only use the doctrine command to check if the doctrine is currently equal to the given value, not less than, or greater than, or any other type of comparison. In all cases, using goals instead of the doctrine will give you more flexibility, but if you run out of available goals then you can use the doctrine like an extra goal if you need it.

The doctrine starts with the value of -1 at the beginning of the game, and it only changes if you use the set-doctrine command.

**参数**：`Value`

*示例：Check if the doctrine is equal to 99.*
```
(defrule
	(doctrine 99)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`goal`, `set-doctrine`, `set-goal`, `up-compare-goal`, `up-modify-goal`

### `dropsite-min-distance`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Economy · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks computer player's minimum dropsite walking distance for a given resource type.**

Checks computer player's minimum dropsite walking distance for a given resource type. The distance is the tile distance between the tile the resource is on and the center tile of the nearest dropsite. For example, if the dropsite is adjacent to the given resource, then dropsite-min-distance will be 1. Long walking distances indicate a need for a new dropsite. It is not recommended to use this fact for building of first dropsites necessary for age advancement. If, at the beginning, the resources happen to be close enough to the Town Center, building of the first dropsites will be delayed, resulting in slower age progression.
If no resources of the given type have been found, then dropsite-min-distance will be -1 for that resource. If resources of the given type have been found but are unaccessible because they are on a different island, then dropsite-min-distance will be 255 for that resource. However, hunting, boar-hunting, deer-hunting, and live-boar drop distances are 255 instead when those hunting resources haven't been found, at least on UP.

There are eight different types of resource dropsite distances you can check for:

food: all food sources, including farms, except boar and fish for fishing ships. Some specially generated objects, like the Incan starting llama, aren't counted.wood: all trees.stone: all stone mines.gold: all gold mines.hunting: all boar and deer (and their geographical variants), both live and dead.boar-hunting: all boar (and their geographical variants), both live and dead, excludes all types of deer.deer-hunting: all deer (and their geographical variants), both live and dead, excludes all types of boar.live-boar: all boar (and their geographical variants) that have not yet been killed.

**参数**：`Resource` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If we have at least one lumber camp and can build another one, build a new lumber camp if the drop distance is > 4 and < 255 (in case only available wood is on a different island).*
```
(defrule
	(building-type-count lumber-camp > 0)
	(can-build lumber-camp)
	(dropsite-min-distance wood > 4)
	(dropsite-min-distance wood < 255)
=>
	(build lumber-camp)
)
```

相关命令：`dropsite-min-distance`, `resource-found`, `sheep-and-forage-too-far`, `up-delete-distant-farms`, `up-find-resource`, `up-remaining-boar-amount`

### `enemy-buildings-in-town`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Buildings, Defense · 复杂度 Low*

**Returns true if there are sighted enemy buildings less than sn-maximum-town-size tiles of the computer player's home TC.**

Returns true if there are sighted enemy buildings less than [sn-maximum-town-size](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-maximum-town-size) tiles of the computer player's home TC. For this fact, sn-maximum-town-size is a circle of sn-maximum-town-size tiles in a diagonal direction and sn-maximum-town-size * sqrt(2) tiles in any straight direction (it appears a perfect circle on the map rather than a square as for the building commands). Works with all buildings (including walls). Updates every few AOC seconds.

*示例：If there are enemy buildings in the computer player's town and it can train a battering ram, train a battering ram.*
```
(defrule
	(enemy-buildings-in-town)
	(can-train battering-ram-line)
=>
	(train battering-ram-line)
)
```

相关命令：`enemy-buildings-in-town`, `town-under-attack`, `up-building-type-in-town`, `up-enemy-buildings-in-town`, `up-enemy-units-in-town`, `up-enemy-villagers-in-town`, `up-set-defense-priority`, `up-unit-type-in-town`, `up-villager-type-in-town`

相关 SN：`sn-allow-civilian-defense`, `sn-allow-civilian-offense`, `sn-disable-defend-groups`, `sn-maximum-town-size`, `sn-safe-town-size`

### `enemy-captured-relics`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Game Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks if the enemy has captured all relics.**

Checks if the enemy team has captured all relics. When this happens, tactical AI automatically starts targeting monasteries and monks. Use this fact to intensify attacks and combine it with the [attack-now](/commands/commands-details.html#attack-now) action to force attacks. You can also add [sn-special-attack-type1](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-special-attack-type1) to 1, [sn-special-attack-influence1](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-special-attack-influence1) > 0, and [up-set-offense-priority](/commands/commands-details.html#up-set-offense-priority) for monasteries to a high number to increase the likelyhood to target monasteries.

*示例：If the enemy team has captured all relics, then set several settings to target monasteries.*
```
(defrule
	(enemy-captured-relics)
=>
	(set-strategic-number sn-special-attack-type1 1)
	(set-strategic-number sn-special-attack-influence1 32767)
	(up-set-offense-priority c: monastery c: 11)
)
```

相关命令：`game-type`, `hold-relics`, `victory-condition`, `up-get-victory-data`

相关 SN：`sn-special-attack-influence1`, `sn-special-attack-type1`

### `escrow-amount`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Economy · 复杂度 Medium*

**Checks a computer player's escrow stockpile amount for a given resource type.**

Checks a computer player's escrow stockpile amount for a given resource type.

AIs can store each of their four resource stockpiles in one of two stockpile types: normal and escrow. Resources in the normal stockpiles are free for the AI to use, while resources in the escrow stockpiles can only be used with [up-build](/commands/commands-details.html#up-build), [up-train](/commands/commands-details.html#up-train), or [up-research](/commands/commands-details.html#up-research) when the EscrowGoalId parameter in these commands is a goal set to the value "with-escrow". The user interface shows the sum of both the normal and escrow stockpile resources added together for each resource.

By default, all resources are stored in the normal stockpiles. However, [set-escrow-percentage](/commands/commands-details.html#set-escrow-percentage) and [up-modify-escrow](/commands/commands-details.html#up-modify-escrow) can be used to store some or all of the AI's resources in the escrow stockpiles instead. Resources in the escrow stockpiles can transferred back into the normal stockpiles by using [release-escrow](/commands/commands-details.html#release-escrow), [up-release-escrow](/commands/commands-details.html#up-release-escrow), or [up-modify-escrow](/commands/commands-details.html#up-modify-escrow).

Resources are usually placed in escrow stockpiles in order to save up for expensive technologies or important buildings or units, so that it isn't spent on lower priority things.

**参数**：`Resource` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Send a chat message if the AI has more than 1000 wood stored in the wood escrow stockpile.*
```
(defrule
	(escrow-amount wood > 1000)
=>
	(chat-local-to-self "We have more than 1000 escrow wood.")
)
```

相关命令：`food-amount`, `gold-amount`, `release-escrow`, `set-escrow-percentage`, `stone-amount`, `wood-amount`, `up-modify-escrow`, `up-release-escrow`, `up-resource-amount`

### `event-detected`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Scenarios · 复杂度 Medium*

**Checks if the given event has been detected.**

Checks if the given event has been detected. Scenario triggers that execute an AI Script Goal effect are the only events that AI scripts can detect. The event-detected fact stays true until the event is explicitly disabled by the [acknowledge-event](/commands/commands-details.html#acknowledge-event) action.

This command, along with [acknowledge-event](/commands/commands-details.html#acknowledge-event), is used to detect an AI Script Goal effect from a scenario trigger, often with the intention of changing the AI behavior after the scenario trigger has fired. The scenario designer chooses an AI Trigger number for the AI Script Goal effect in the scenario editor. Then, the event-detected command in the AI script will detect when this trigger effect happens. The event-detected command will remain true after the AI Script Goal trigger effect fires, so acknowledge-event is used to reset the event-detected flag so that event-detected will no longer be true, similar to how the [disable-timer](/commands/commands-details.html#disable-timer) command clears a timer that has triggered or how the [acknowledge-taunt](/commands/commands-details.html#acknowledge-taunt) command accepts the taunt message.

Trigger events are essentially the inverse of signals. To allow an AI script to send a signal which the AI Signal trigger condition can detect, use [set-signal](/commands/commands-details.html#set-signal).

**参数**：`EventType` — `EventId`

*示例：If an AI Script Goal trigger effect has fired with AI trigger 36, detect it with event-detected and acknowledge it with acknowledge-event. gl-trigger-event-fired is set to 1 so that (goal gl-trigger-event-fired 1) can be used as a condition to change the AI behavior in the scenario after the AI Script Goal trigger effect has fired.*
```
(defconst gl-trigger-effect-fired 101)
(defrule
	(event-detected trigger 36)
=>
	(set-goal gl-trigger-effect-fired 1)
	(acknowledge-event trigger 36)
)
```

相关命令：`acknowledge-event`, `set-signal`, `up-get-event`, `up-get-signal`, `up-set-event`, `up-set-signal`

### `false`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Other · 复杂度 Low*

**A Fact that is always false. A rule with this fact will never execute its actions.**

A Fact that is always false. A rule with this fact will never execute its actions.

This command was likely added to the AI engine by Ensemble Studios early on to test logical operators, such as "or," "and," or "not." This command's usefulness is pretty limited, but scripters might be able to use it creatively. If you want to stop a rule from running, a more effective strategy is to comment out each of the lines in the rule with a semi-colon (;).

*示例：The attack-now command in this rule will never execute, even if the AI has more than ten military pop.*
```
(defrule
	(military-population > 10)
	(false)
=>
	(attack-now)
)
```

相关命令：`true`

### `fe-idle-pasture-count`
*版本 DE · 分类 Counting, Economy · 复杂度 Low*

**DE only. Checks the number of pastures with zero herders gathering from it.**

DE only. Checks the number of pastures with zero herders gathering from it. It can be used before a new pasture is built to make sure it is needed.

To check the number of idle farms, use [idle-farm-count](/commands/commands-details.html#idle-farm-count).

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If we have no idle pastures and have less than 40 pastures, build a new pasture if we can.*
```
(defconst pasture 1889)
(defconst pasture-mangrove 1893) ;pastures built on mangrove terrain
(defconst pasture-land 1897) ;pastures built on land terrain
(defrule
	(fe-idle-pasture-count == 0)
	(building-type-count-total pasture-mangrove < 40)
	(building-type-count-total pasture-land < 40)
	(can-build pasture)
=>
	(build pasture)
)
```

相关命令：`idle-farm-count`, `up-idle-unit-count`

相关 SN：`sn-maximum-food-drop-distance`

### `fe-sub-game-type`
*版本 DE · 分类 Game Info · 复杂度 Medium*

**DE only. Checks if game matches the specified sub-game type.**

DE only. Checks if game matches the specified sub-game type. There are four sub-game types: sub-game-type-empire-wars, sub-game-type-sudden-death, sub-game-type-regicide, and sub-game-type-king-of-the-hill. Sub-games are loaded whenever the checkbox for these sub-game modes are checked in the lobby screen, rather than being selected from the game type dropdown. Multiple sub-games modes can be true at once in a game.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `SubGameType`

*示例：Check if players can win by being the last player with a king still alive. If so, set castles as highest priority.*
```
(defrule
	(fe-sub-game-type == sub-game-type-regicide)
=>
	(set-strategic-number sn-enable-offensive-priority 1)
	(up-set-offense-priority c: castle c: 11)
	(up-set-defense-priority c: castle c: 32767)
)
```

相关命令：`game-type`, `regicide-game`, `victory-condition`

### `food-amount`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Economy · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks a computer player's food amount.**

Checks a computer player's food amount. This amount includes escrowed food.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If we have at least 2000 food and can sell food, then sell food.*
```
(defrule
	(food-amount >= 2000)
	(can-sell-commodity food)
=>
	(sell-commodity food)
)
```

相关命令：`gold-amount`, `stone-amount`, `wood-amount`, `up-allied-resource-amount`, `up-resource-amount`

### `game-time`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Game Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the game time.**

Checks the game time, the amount of time elapsed since the start of the game, measured in game seconds. The fact can be used to make rules time-specific. For example, the computer can become more aggressive after 15 minutes of game time.

game-time measures the game time in game seconds. The current game time can be found by using F11. Unless the game is being played on Slow speed (1.0 speed), the game time moves faster that real time. Casual speed (Normal speed in single player games in UP) is 1.5 times faster than real time, Normal speed (Normal speed in multiplayer games in UP) is 1.7 times faster than real time, and Fast speed is 2.0 times faster than real time. Even faster speeds are possible with DE launch options (see this [video](https://youtu.be/jGaaQ6FmxiU) or with the speedhack option in Cheat Engine, though AI behavior can diminish at faster game speeds.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Send a one-time attack twenty minutes (1200 seconds) into the game.*
```
(defrule
	(game-time > 1200)
=>
	(attack-now)
	(disable-self)
)
```

相关命令：`current-age-time`, `players-current-age-time`, `up-get-precise-time`

相关 SN：`sn-home-exploration-time`

### `game-type`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Game Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the game type.**

Checks the game type. Game types include settings like random-map, regicide, king-of-the-hill, or turbo-random-map. See [GameType](/parameters/parameters-details.html#GameType) for the list of game types. Game types are not defined, so you must defconst them before using them.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `GameType`

*示例：Check if a turbo random map game is being played.*
```
(defconst turbo-random-map 8)
(defrule
	(game-type == turbo-random-map)
=>
	(disable-self)
)
```

相关命令：`death-match-game`, `regicide-game`, `victory-condition`, `fe-sub-game-type`

### `gate-count`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Buildings, Counting · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks for the number of gates that are either being built or are completed at the given perimeter.**

Checks for the number of gates that are either being built or are completed at the given perimeter. Perimeter 1 is usually between 10 and 20 tiles from the starting Town Center. Perimeter 2 is usually between 18 and 30 tiles from the starting Town Center.

This command likely only counts stone gates, but it is possible that you can count only palisade gates instead by setting [sn-gate-type-for-wall](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-gate-type-for-wall) to 1 before using gate-count.

**参数**：`Perimeter` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If there are no stone gates at perimeter 2, build a stone gate at perimeter 2.*
```
(defrule
	(gate-count 2 == 0)
	(can-build-gate 2)
=>
	(build-gate 2)
)
```

相关命令：`building-type-count`, `building-type-count-total`

### `goal`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Goals · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the current value of the given goal.**

Checks the current value of the given goal to see if it is equal to the given value. To do any other comparisons, including comparisons with goal or strategic number values, use [up-compare-goal](/commands/commands-details.html#up-compare-goal).

While their purpose may be unclear based on their name, goals are variables which can store an integer value which can be checked with this command or with [up-compare-goal](/commands/commands-details.html#up-compare-goal). Each goal is given an ID, and AIs have 16000 goals available (only 512 in UP and only 40 in AoC) that they can use to store different values, and they all store the value -1 at the beginning of the game. Goals are one of the most important concepts of AI scripting, so it's good to learn how to use them.

In programming speak, goals are a 16000-length one-indexed 32-bit integer array, pre-initialized to -1, and a GoalId refers to a particular index of that array. The [goal](/commands/commands-details.html#goal) command checks if the value of the given GoalId is equal to the given value. New goals or variables cannot be defined, only constants (called defconsts by the AI engine), so AI scripters are limited to these 16000 goals, though unused strategic numbers can also be used like goals in a pinch.

If the paragraph above makes absolutely no sense to you, you can imagine goals like a bank which holds 16000 bank accounts, numbered with IDs from 1 to 16000. These accounts can hold whole amounts (no cents or decimal amounts of money), and they can store either positive or negative amounts of money. These bank accounts are restricted to holding between -2,147,483,648 and 2,147,483,647 dollars, and they all start with -$1 (negative 1 dollars) stored inside them until they are used by a customer (the AI scripter). The [set-goal](/commands/commands-details.html#set-goal) and [up-modify-goal](/commands/commands-details.html#up-modify-goal) commands can modify how much money is stored in a particular account.

Following this bank metaphor, the [goal](/commands/commands-details.html#goal) command checks if the given bank account number holds the given amount of money. For example, (goal 5 13) checks if goal ID #5 holds the value 13 (i.e. bank account #5 holds $13), and (goal 415 -3274) checks if goal ID #415 holds the value -3,274 (i.e. bank account #415 holds -$3,274). You can also use [up-compare-goal](/commands/commands-details.html#up-compare-goal) to check the current value of a goal ID in a more powerful manner, such as checking if the goal stores greater or less than the given value.

It is pretty common to use a defconst to refer to a goal ID number to make the AI more readable. See the second example below on what this looks like.

**参数**：`GoalId` — `Value`

*示例：Check if goal ID 50 is set to the value 1. If so, attack once.*
```
(defrule
	(goal 50 1)
=>
	(attack-now)
	(disable-self)
)
```

*示例：Use a defconst to refer to goal ID 50 and check if the goal is set to the value 1. If so, attack once. This rule is functionally identical to the above example, because the AI engine will substitute the defconsted value of gl-attack-now (50) everywhere it sees gl-attack-now in the script before running the AI.*
```
(defconst gl-attack-now 50)
(defrule
	(goal gl-attack-now 1)
=>
	(attack-now)
	(disable-self)
)
```

相关命令：`doctrine`, `set-doctrine`, `set-goal`, `set-shared-goal`, `shared-goal`, `up-allied-goal`, `up-compare-goal`, `up-compare-flag`, `up-get-shared-goal`, `up-get-indirect-goal`, `up-modify-flag`, `up-modify-goal`, `up-set-indirect-goal`

### `gold-amount`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Economy · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks a computer player's gold amount.**

Checks a computer player's gold amount. This amount includes escrowed gold.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If we have at least 1000 gold and less than 200 food, then buy food.*
```
(defrule
	(gold-amount >= 1000)
	(food-amount < 200)
	(can-buy-commodity food)
=>
	(buy-commodity food)
)
```

相关命令：`food-amount`, `stone-amount`, `wood-amount`, `up-allied-resource-amount`, `up-resource-amount`

### `hold-koh-ruin`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Game Info, Own Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Undocumented command that checks whether or not it (or its team) currently holds the monument in King of the Hill games.**

Undocumented command that checks whether or not it (or its team) currently holds the monument in King of the Hill games. Koh stands for King of the Kill.

*示例：If we (or our team) have captured the monument, set [sn-gather-idle-soldiers-at-center](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-gather-idle-soldiers-at-center) to 1.*
```
(defrule
	(hold-koh-run)
=>
	(set-strategic-number sn-gather-idle-soldiers-at-center 1)
)
```

相关命令：`enemy-captured-relics`, `hold-relics`, `up-get-victory-data`

### `hold-relics`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Game Info, Own Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Undocumented command that checks whether or not it (or its team) has all of the relics.**

*示例：Check whether we (or our team) has captured all the relics.*
```
(defrule
	(hold-relics)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`enemy-captured-relics`, `hold-koh-ruin`, `up-get-victory-data`

### `housing-headroom`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Own Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks computer player's housing headroom, the difference between current housing capacity and the current population.**

Checks computer player's housing headroom. Housing headroom is the difference between current housing capacity and the current population. For example, a computer player has a Town Center (capacity 5), a House (capacity 5) and 6 villagers. In this case, housing headroom is 4.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If our housing headroom is less than 2 and the population headroom is > 0, build a house if we can.*
```
(defrule
	(housing-headroom < 2)
	(population-headroom > 0)
	(can-build house)
=>
	(build house)
)
```

相关命令：`population-headroom`, `population`, `up-resource-amount`

### `idle-farm-count`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Counting, Economy · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks a computer player's idle farm count - the number of farms with no farmers assigned to gather from it.**

Checks a computer player's idle farm count - the number of farms with no farmers assigned to gather from it. It can be used before a new farm is built to make sure it is needed.

To check the number of idle pastures, use [fe-idle-pasture-count](/commands/commands-details.html#fe-idle-pasture-count).

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If we have no idle farms, build a new farm if we can.*
```
(defrule
	(idle-farm-count == 0)
	(can-build farm)
=>
	(build farm)
)
```

相关命令：`up-delete-distant-farms`, `up-idle-unit-count`, `fe-idle-pasture-count`

相关 SN：`sn-maximum-food-drop-distance`

### `map-size`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Game Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the map size.**

Checks the map size. The map sizes can be tiny, small, medium, normal, large, giant, or ludikris (DE only). To get the actual dimensions of the map, you can use [up-get-point](/commands/commands-details.html#up-get-point) with position-map-size, which will store the coordinates of the rightmost point on the map.

**参数**：`MapSize`

*示例：If the map is a large map, get the coordinates of the rightmost point on the map.*
```
(defconst gl-map-size-x 200)
(defconst gl-map-size-y 201)
(defrule
	(map-size large)
=>
	(up-get-point position-map-size gl-map-size-x)
)
```

相关命令：`map-type`, `up-get-point`

相关 SN：`sn-initial-exploration-required`, `sn-percent-exploration-required`, `sn-percent-half-exploration`

### `map-type`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Game Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the map type.**

Checks the map type. The map type is the map's name. See [MapType](/parameters/parameters-details.html#MapType) for a complete list of maps.

For custom random maps, the map type is "custom_map" (yes, with the underscore). The exception is if the custom random map script uses ai_info_map_type. For example, if the random map script has ai_info_map_type ARABIA 0 0 0, then (map-type arabia) will be true instead of (map-type custom_map).

**参数**：`MapType`

*示例：Check if the map type is Arena.*
```
(defrule
	(map-type arena)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`map-size`, `up-store-map-name`

### `military-population`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Counting, Units · 复杂度 Low*

**Check's the player's military population.**

Check's the player's military population. Military population includes any units that aren't civilian population (not villagers, trade units and fishing ships). It includes transport ships, but it does not count kings.

To check for the military-population of other players, use [players-military-population](/commands/commands-details.html#players-military-population). This command counts Karambit Warriors as 1 population, rather than 0.5 population.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If military population is < 10 and we can build a castle, build a castle.*
```
(defrule
	(military-population < 10)
	(can-build castle)
=>
	(build castle)
)
```

相关命令：`attack-soldier-count`, `attack-warboat-count`, `civilian-population`, `defend-soldier-count`, `defend-warboat-count`, `players-military-population`, `population`, `soldier-count`, `warboat-count`

相关 SN：`sn-percent-attack-boats`, `sn-percent-attack-soldiers`

### `player-computer`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks if the given player is a computer player.**

Checks if the given player is a computer player.

The fact allows "my-player-number", "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber`

*示例：Check if the target player is a computer player instead of a human player.*
```
(defrule
	(player-computer target-player)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`player-human`

### `player-human`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks if the given player is a human player.**

Checks if the given player is a human player.

The fact allows "my-player-number", "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber`

*示例：Check if the focus player is a human player instead of a computer player.*
```
(defrule
	(player-human focus-player)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`player-computer`

### `player-in-game`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks if the given player is a valid player and still playing.**

Checks if the given player is a valid player and still playing (hasn't resigned or been defeated).

The fact allows "my-player-number", "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber`

*示例：Train up to 40 trade carts if an ally is in the game and the AI has reached Imperial Age.*
```
(defrule
	(player-in-game any-ally)
	(current-age == imperial-age)
	(unit-type-count-total trade-cart < 40)
	(can-train trade-cart)
=>
	(train trade-cart)
)
```

相关命令：`player-resigned`, `player-valid`, `up-players-in-game`

### `player-number`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks computer player's player number.**

Checks computer player's player number. The player number is the player's slot order, not the number associated with the AI's player color. Only a number between 1 to 8 can be used. It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber`

*示例：Check if the AI is playing as player 2.*
```
(defrule
	(player-number 2)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`up-find-player`, `up-find-next-player`, `up-get-player-color`

相关 SN：`sn-focus-player-number`, `sn-target-player-number`

### `player-resigned`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks if the given player has lost by resigning.**

Checks if the given player has lost by resigning. Note that a player can lose without resigning, so this fact should not be used to check whether a player has lost a game. To check whether a player has lost a game (such as player 3) use:
(not (player-in-game 3))The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber`

*示例：Send "gg" if player 3 resigned and is an enemy.*
```
(defrule
	(player-resigned 3)
	(stance-toward 3 enemy)
=>
	(chat-to-enemies "gg")
	(disable-self)
)
```

相关命令：`player-in-game`, `player-valid`, `resign`, `up-players-in-game`

### `player-valid`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks if the given player is a valid player.**

Checks if the given player is a valid player, meaning the player slot was used during the game. In games with more than 2 players, players that lost before the game is over are still considered to be valid players. This is because although the player is not in the game, their units/buildings can still be in the game. To check whether the given player is still in the game use the [player-in-game](/commands/commands-details.html#player-in-game) fact.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber`

*示例：Check if we're playing in a game with more than 2 players by checking if player 3 is valid.*
```
(defrule
	(player-valid 3)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`player-in-game`, `player-resigned`, `up-players-in-game`

### `players-building-count`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Buildings, Counting, Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the given player's building count. Both existing buildings and buildings under construction are included.**

Checks the given player's building count. Both existing buildings and buildings under construction are included. The computer player relies only on what it has seen - no cheating. If [sn-coop-share-information](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-coop-share-information) is set to its default value of 1, any buildings seen by allies are also counted. A cheating version of this command, [cc-players-building-count](/commands/commands-details.html#cc-players-building-count), can be used to count any building of this type, whether it has been scouted or not.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber), and the use of building line wildcard parameters for [BuildingId](/parameters/parameters-details.html#BuildingId). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If an enemy building has been explored, and we have at least 12 military, increase [sn-maximum-town-size](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-maximum-town-size) by 2 each pass if enemy buildings aren't in our town. This will initiate a Town Size Attack (TSA), where the AI attacks enemy buildings that are within its sn-maximum-town-size in order to "defend" its town.*
```
(defrule
	(players-building-count any-enemy > 0)
	(military-population > 12)
	(up-enemy-buildings-in-town == 0)
	(strategic-number sn-maximum-town-size < 255)
=>
	(up-modify-sn sn-maximum-town-size c:+ 2)
)
```

相关命令：`building-count`, `building-count-total`, `cc-players-building-count`, `cc-players-building-type-count`, `players-building-type-count`

相关 SN：`sn-coop-share-information`

### `players-building-type-count`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Buildings, Counting, Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the given player's building count of the given type.**

Checks the given player's building count of the given type. Both existing buildings and buildings under construction of the given type are included. The computer player relies only on what it has seen - no cheating. If [sn-coop-share-information](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-coop-share-information) is set to its default value of 1, any buildings seen by allies are also counted. A cheating version of this command, [cc-players-building-type-count](/commands/commands-details.html#cc-players-building-type-count), can be used to count any building, whether it has been scouted or not.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber), and the use of building line wildcard parameters for [BuildingId](/parameters/parameters-details.html#BuildingId). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `BuildingId` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If an ally has a market and the AI has less than 40 trade carts, train a trade cart.*
```
(defrule
	(players-building-type-count any-ally market > 0)
	(unit-type-count-total trade-cart < 40)
	(can-train trade-cart)
=>
	(train trade-cart)
)
```

相关命令：`building-count`, `building-count-total`, `cc-players-building-count`, `cc-players-building-type-count`, `players-building-count`

相关 SN：`sn-coop-share-information`

### `players-civ`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the given player's civilization.**

Checks the given player's civilization.

Note that the civilization names used with this command for pre-DE civs are usually different than the civ's display name. They are like the [LoadIfSymbol](/parameters/parameters-details.html#LoadIfSymbol) civ names where they often use the adjective form of the civ name, not the plural name. See [Civ](/parameters/parameters-details.html#Civ) for a list of correct civ names to use with this command.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1). You can use "my-civ" for the Civ parameter, which will automatically detect the civilization the AI is playing as.

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `Civ`

*示例：Check if any ally players are playing as the Spanish.*
```
(defrule
	(players-civ any-ally spanish)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

*示例：Check if any enemy players are playing as the same civilization.*
```
(defrule
	(players-civ any-enemy my-civ)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`civ-selected`

### `players-civilian-population`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Counting, Other Player Info, Units · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks a given player's civilian population.**

Checks a given player's civilian population, which includes villagers, fishing ships, and trade units. This fact includes seen and unseen civilians for the given player.

The CPSB notes that this is equivalent to a human player checking the timeline, which was possible in-game in AoE1, and it was probably also possible during AoE2 development when the CPSB was written, hence why this isn't regarded as a cc- cheating command. However, since this command includes unseen civilian units, some consider this command to be cheating when it's used to check enemy civilian population, but the AI scripting community permits this command in AI tournaments for historical reasons.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if an ally has less than 50 civilian population.*
```
(defrule
	(players-civilian-population any-ally < 50)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`civilian-population`, `players-military-population`, `players-population`

### `players-current-age`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the given player's current age.**

Checks the given player's current age. The CPSB notes that this is equivalent to a human player checking the timeline, which was possible in-game in AoE1, and it was probably also possible during AoE2 development when the CPSB was written. Of course, this information is available to all players in-game, even without the timeline.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `compareOp` — `Age`

*示例：Check if every enemy player has advanced to the Castle Age.*
```
(defrule
	(players-current-age every-enemy >= castle-age)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`current-age`, `current-age-time`, `players-current-age-time`

### `players-current-age-time`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the given player's current age time -- time spent in the current age.**

Checks the given player's current age time -- time spent in the current age. The CPSB notes that this is equivalent to a human player checking the timeline, which was possible in-game in AoE1, and it was probably also possible during AoE2 development when the CPSB was written. Of course, this information could be calculated in-game even without using the timeline.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if an enemy player has spent less than a minute (60 seconds) in their current age.*
```
(defrule
	(players-current-age-time any-enemy < 60)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`current-age`, `current-age-time`, `players-current-age`

### `players-military-population`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Counting, Other Player Info, Units · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the given player's military population.**

Checks the given player's military population, which includes all units except for villagers, fishing ships, trade units, and kings. This fact includes seen and unseen military units for the given player. This command counts Karambit Warriors as 1 population, rather than 0.5 population.

The CPSB notes that this is equivalent to a human player checking the timeline, which was possible in-game in AoE1, and it was probably also possible during AoE2 development when the CPSB was written, hence why this isn't regarded as a cc- cheating command. However, since this command includes unseen military units, some consider this command to be cheating when it's used to check enemy military population, but the AI scripting community permits this command in AI tournaments for historical reasons.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if an ally has less than 12 military units.*
```
(defrule
	(players-military-population any-ally < 12)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`military-population`, `players-civilian-population`, `players-population`

### `players-population`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Counting, Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the given player's population.**

Checks the given player's population. This fact includes seen and unseen units for the given player. This command counts Karambit Warriors as 1 population, rather than 0.5 population.

The CPSB notes that this is equivalent to a human player checking the timeline, which was possible in-game in AoE1, and it was probably also possible during AoE2 development when the CPSB was written, hence why this isn't regarded as a cc- cheating command. However, since this command includes unseen units, some consider this command to be cheating when it's used to check enemy population, but the AI scripting community permits this command in AI tournaments for historical reasons. When checking for the population of an enemy player, consider using [players-unit-count](/commands/commands-details.html#players-unit-count). players-unit-count can overestimate enemy unit counts, but it doesn't count unseen units.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if every ally has more than 120 population.*
```
(defrule
	(players-population every-ally > 120)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`players-civilian-population`, `players-military-population`, `population`

### `players-score`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the given player's current score.**

Checks the given player's current score.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if an enemy player has a score more than 9,000.*
```
(defrule
	(players-score any-enemy > 9000)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`current-score`, `victory-condition`, `up-get-victory-limit`

相关 SN：`sn-attack-winning-player`, `sn-attack-winning-player-factor`

### `players-stance`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Diplomacy, Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks if the given player's diplomatic stance toward the computer player matches the given stance.**

Checks if the given player's diplomatic stance toward the computer player matches the give stance, either ally, neutral, or enemy. To check our stance toward another player, use [stance-toward](/commands/commands-details.html#stance-toward).

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `PlayerStance`

*示例：If a player we are allied to has set their stance toward us to enemy, change our stance toward them to enemy.*
```
(defrule
	(players-stance any-ally enemy)
=>
	(set-stance this-any-ally enemy)
)
```

相关命令：`set-stance`, `stance-toward`

### `players-tribute`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Diplomacy, Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the player's tribute given throughout the game.**

Checks the player's tribute given throughout the game. Only tribute for the given resource type is checked.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `Resource` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If we have less than 200 food and an ally has tributed less than 500 food to us throughout the game, set this ally as the focus-player, check if they have > 180 population, and ask for food.*
```
(defconst gl-player 100)
(defrule
	(food-amount < 200)
	(players-tribute any-ally food < 500)
=>
	(up-get-player-fact this-any-ally player-number 0 gl-player)
	(up-modify-sn sn-focus-player-number g:= gl-player)
)

(defrule
	(food-amount < 200)
	(players-tribute focus-player food < 500)
	(players-population focus-player > 180)
=>
	(chat-to-player focus-player "3Food Please")
)
```

*示例：In case anyone is wondering, this simpler example below would not be equivalent to the example above, because the players-tribute and players-population conditions could be true for different allies. And you couldn't try to fix this by using this-any-ally with players-population instead of any-ally, because the this-any-* rule variables don't work with the vast majority of fact commands. They only work with some action commands.*
```
(defrule
	(food-amount < 200)
	(players-tribute any-ally food < 500)
	(players-population any-ally > 180)
=>
	(chat-to-player this-any-ally "3Food Please")
)
```

相关命令：`clear-tribute-memory`, `players-tribute-memory`, `tribute-to-player`, `up-tribute-to-player`

### `players-tribute-memory`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Diplomacy, Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks a player's tribute given since the player's tribute memory for the given resource was cleared.**

Checks a player's tribute given since the player's tribute memory for the given resource was cleared. Only tribute memory for the given resource type is checked. The tribute memory is cleared by using [clear-tribute-memory](/commands/commands-details.html#clear-tribute-memory).

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `Resource` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if any ally has tributed gold to us since the tribute memory for that player was cleared.*
```
(defrule
	(players-tribute-memory any-ally gold > 0)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`clear-tribute-memory`, `players-tribute`, `tribute-to-player`, `up-tribute-to-player`

### `players-unit-count`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Counting, Other Player Info, Units · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the given player's unit count. The computer player relies only on what it has seen - no cheating.**

Checks the given player's unit count. The computer player relies only on what it has seen - no cheating. For allies and self only trained units are included.

To count the units of other players, each AI internally stores an explored objects list which is used by the players-unit-count, [players-unit-type-count](/commands/commands-details.html#players-unit-type-count), [players-building-count](/commands/commands-details.html#players-building-count), and [players-building-type-count](/commands/commands-details.html#players-building-type-count) commands. Every time the AI sees an object owned by another player, that object is added to this list. However, while explored ally objects that have died are immediately removed from the explored objects list, the AI doesn't remove enemy objects from this list when they die because the object might have died in the fog of war without the AI's knowledge. Instead, the AI clears their explored objects list periodically, about every five minutes. Setting [sn-coop-share-information](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-coop-share-information) allows AIs to read the explored object lists of ally players. Researching Cartography, turning on the Shared Exploration game setting in DE, and researching Spies likely don't change how the explored object list system works. The possible presence of dead enemy objects in the explored object list means that players-unit-count may overcount the actual unit counts of enemy players.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if we have explored at least 50 enemy units owned by an enemy player.*
```
(defrule
	(players-unit-type-count any-enemy >= 50)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`players-population`, `players-unit-type-count`, `unit-count`, `unit-count-total`

相关 SN：`sn-coop-share-information`

### `players-unit-type-count`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Counting, Other Player Info, Units · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the given player's unit count of the given type. The computer player relies only on what it has seen - no cheating.**

Checks the given player's unit count of the given type. The computer player relies only on what it has seen - no cheating. For allies and self only trained units of the given type are included.

my-unique-unit, my-elite-unique-unit, and my-unique-unit-line can also be used if you're counting the AI's own units, which will automatically get the UnitId of the unique unit, elite unique unit, or unique unit line that the AI's civ can train from the castle. However, to count the AI's own units, consider using [unit-type-count](/commands/commands-details.html#unit-type-count) or [unit-type-count-total](/commands/commands-details.html#unit-type-count-total) instead.

Important Note: players-unit-type-count cannot use classes to count enemy units, such as (players-unit-type-count any-enemy cavalry-class > 5), though you can use classes to count allied units or the computer player's own units.

To count the units of other players, each AI internally stores an explored objects list which is used by the [players-unit-count](/commands/commands-details.html#players-unit-count), players-unit-type-count, [players-building-count](/commands/commands-details.html#players-building-count), and [players-building-type-count](/commands/commands-details.html#players-building-type-count) commands. Every time the AI sees an object owned by another player, that object is added to this list. However, while explored ally objects that have died are immediately removed from the explored objects list, the AI doesn't remove enemy objects from this list when they die because the object might have died in the fog of war without the AI's knowledge. Instead, the AI clears their explored objects list periodically, about every five minutes. Setting [sn-coop-share-information](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-coop-share-information) allows AIs to read the explored object lists of ally players. Researching Cartography, turning on the Shared Exploration game setting in DE, and researching Spies likely don't change how the explored object list system works. The possible presence of dead enemy objects in the explored object list means that players-unit-type-count may overcount the actual unit counts of enemy players.

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `UnitId` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if an ally player has at least 20 trade carts.*
```
(defrule
	(players-unit-type-count any-ally trade-cart > 20)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

*示例：This example won't work because you can't count enemy units with classes. Use the last example to count enemy cavalry archers.*
```
(defrule
	(players-unit-type-count any-enemy cavalry-archer-class > 10)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

*示例：Check if any enemy player has at least 10 cavalry archer type units, not including unique units.*
```
(defrule
	(or
		(players-unit-type-count any-enemy cavalry-archer-line > 10)
		(or
			(players-unit-type-count any-enemy genitour-line > 10)
			(players-unit-type-count any-enemy elephant-archer-line > 10)))
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`players-unit-count`, `unit-type-count`, `unit-type-count-total`

相关 SN：`sn-coop-share-information`

### `population`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Counting, Own Player Info, Units · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the computer player's population.**

Checks the computer player's population.

To check for the population of other players, use [players-population](/commands/commands-details.html#players-population). This command counts Karambit Warriors as 1 population, rather than 0.5 population.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if our population is over 100.*
```
(defrule
	(population > 100)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`civilian-population`, `military-population`, `players-population`, `unit-count`

### `population-cap`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Game Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the population cap setting.**

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if the population cap is less than 200.*
```
(defrule
	(population-cap < 200)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`population`, `population-headroom`

### `population-headroom`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Counting, Own Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the computer player's population headroom.**

Checks the computer player's population headroom. Population headroom is the difference between the game's population cap and current housing capacity. For example, in a game with a population cap of 75, if the computer player has a town center (capacity 5) and a house (capacity 5), then the population headroom is 65.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If the housing headroom is less than 5 and the population headroom is more than zero, build a house.*
```
(defrule
	(housing-headroom < 5)
	(population-headroom > 0)
	(can-build house)
=>
	(build house)
)
```

相关命令：`housing-headroom`, `population-cap`

### `random-number`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Other · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks random number value generated by generate-random-number.**

Checks the value of the most recent random number value generated by [generate-random-number](/commands/commands-details.html#generate-random-number). Random numbers can range from 0 to 32767. To store the random number in a goal, use [up-get-fact](/commands/commands-details.html#up-get-fact) with random-number as the [FactId](/parameters/parameters-details.html#FactId).

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：Check if the random number is at least 10.*
```
(defrule
	(random-number >= 10)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`generate-random-number`

### `regicide-game`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Game Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks if the game is a regicide game.**

Checks if the game is a regicide game. You can also enclose code in a #load-if-defined REGICIDE block if it should only run in a regicide game. In DE, to check if the Regicide secondary game mode is active you can use [fe-sub-game-type](/commands/commands-details.html#fe-sub-game-type).

*示例：If the game is a regicide game, the AI will chat taunt 17 ("It is good to be the king").*
```
(defrule
	(regicide-game)
=>
	(chat-to-all "17")
	(disable-self)
)
```

相关命令：`death-match-game`, `game-type`, `fe-sub-game-type`

### `research-available`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Can Do, Techs · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks that the given research is available to the computer player's civ, and that the research is available at this time.**

Checks that the given research is available to the computer player's civ, and that the research is available at this time (technology and tech tree prerequisites are met). The fact does not check that there are enough resources to start researching or if the player has built the building needed to research the technology.

Unfortunately, because most technologies have an age as a prerequisite tech, research-available cannot be used at the beginning of the game to check if a technology is available in the civ's tech tree. There currently isn't a command that simply checks whether a technology is available in a civ's tech tree.

Using this command is equivalent to using [up-research-status](/commands/commands-details.html#up-research-status) to check if the technology's research status is equal to research-available.

**参数**：`TechId`

*示例：Checks if Chain Barding Armor is available. The prerequisite techs are Castle Age and Scale Barding Armor, so this will only return true if the AI is in Castle Age or Imperial Age, has researched Scale Barding Armor, hasn't researched Chain Barding Armor, and isn't Aztecs, Incas, Malay, or Mayans. It won't check if the AI can afford Chain Barding Armor or has a blacksmith.*
```
(defrule
	(research-available ri-chain-barding)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`building-available`, `can-afford-research`, `can-research-with-escrow`, `research`, `research-completed`, `unit-available`, `up-can-research`, `up-research`, `up-research-status`

### `research-completed`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Can Do, Techs · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks that the given research is completed.**

Checks that the given research is completed.

Using this command is equivalent to using [up-research-status](/commands/commands-details.html#up-research-status) to check if the technology's research status is equal to research-complete.

**参数**：`TechId`

*示例：If the Caravan technology has been researched, an ally player has a market, and we have less than 40 trade carts, train a trade cart.*
```
(defrule
	(research-completed ri-caravan)
	(players-building-type-count any-ally market > 0)
	(unit-type-count-total trade-cart < 40)
	(can-train trade-cart)
=>
	(train trade-cart)
)
```

相关命令：`can-research-with-escrow`, `research`, `research-available`, `up-can-research`, `up-research`, `up-research-status`

### `resource-found`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Economy · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks whether the computer player has found the given resource.**

Checks whether the computer player has found the given resource. For food, gold, and stone (not wood), the given resource must be within the dropsite's max distance for this command to be true ([sn-mill-max-distance](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-mill-max-distance) for food and [sn-mining-camp-max-distance](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-mining-camp-max-distance) or [sn-camp-max-distance](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-camp-max-distance) for gold and stone). The fact should be used at the beginning period of the game. Once it becomes true for a certain resource it stays true for that resource.

Only forests, not straggler trees, will make resource-found true for wood. Also, only forage bushes will make resource-found true for food.

Using [up-gaia-type-count](/commands/commands-details.html#up-gaia-type-count), [up-gaia-type-count-total](/commands/commands-details.html#up-gaia-type-count-total), or [dropsite-min-distance](/commands/commands-details.html#dropsite-min-distance) are often better commands to use than resource-found because they can count how many of the given resource have been found or determine how far away the resources are.

**参数**：`Resource`

*示例：Check if forage bushes have been found.*
```
(defrule
	(resource-found food)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`dropsite-min-distance`, `sheep-and-forage-too-far`, `up-gaia-type-count`, `up-gaia-type-count-total`, `up-find-resource`

相关 SN：`sn-camp-max-distance`, `sn-mill-max-distance`, `sn-mining-camp-max-distance`

### `shared-goal`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Goals, Other Player Info · 复杂度 Medium*

**Checks a given shared goal (a goal that is shared among all computer players).**

Checks a given shared goal (a goal that is shared among all computer players). It is to be used only when all computer players are on the same team.

Shared goals are a separate set of 256 goals, in addition to the regular 16000 normal goals, which are shared between all AIs in the game, even between AIs that are enemies. Any AI can modify them at any time with [set-shared-goal](/commands/commands-details.html#set-shared-goal) or [up-set-shared-goal](/commands/commands-details.html#up-set-shared-goal), and all AIs can check their values with shared-goal or [up-get-shared-goal](/commands/commands-details.html#up-get-shared-goal). Otherwise, shared goals share the same characteristics of normal goals, which you can read about in the [set-goal](/commands/commands-details.html#set-goal) description.

Because shared goals can change without the AI's knowledge and the fact than enemy AIs can check their values, it's often better to use [up-allied-goal](/commands/commands-details.html#up-allied-goal), which allows you to check the value of one of an allied AI's normal 16000 goals.

**参数**：`SharedGoalId` — `Value`

*示例：If shared goal #31 (defconsted to sgl-coop-attacking) is set to the value 1, attack if we have enough soldiers.*
```
(defconst sgl-coop-attacking 31)
(defrule
	(shared-goal sgl-coop-attacking 1)
	(defend-soldier-count > 12)
=>
	(set-strategic-number sn-number-attack-groups 20)
)
```

相关命令：`goal`, `set-goal`, `set-shared-goal`, `up-allied-goal`, `up-compare-goal`, `up-get-shared-goal`, `up-modify-goal`, `up-set-shared-goal`

### `sheep-and-forage-too-far`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Economy · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks whether the computer player has any forage site(s) and/or sheep nearby.**

Checks whether the computer player has any forage site(s) and/or sheep within 8 tiles of the drop-off location (Mill or Town Center). If not, this fact is true.

To check if any resource is within a certain distance of a dropsite, you can use [dropsite-min-distance](/commands/commands-details.html#dropsite-min-distance) instead, which is usually more flexible. You can check if the AI can currently see any particular resource with [up-gaia-type-count](/commands/commands-details.html#up-gaia-type-count).

*示例：If we don't have any forage sites or sheep within 8 tiles of a TC or mill, build up to 8 farms.*
```
(defrule
	(sheep-and-forage-too-far)
	(building-type-count-total farm < 8)
	(can-build farm)
=>
	(build-farm)
)
```

相关命令：`dropsite-min-distance`, `resource-found`, `up-gaia-type-count`, `up-gaia-type-count-total`, `up-find-resource`, `up-remaining-boar-amount`

### `soldier-count`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Counting, Units · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the computer player's soldier count. A soldier is a land-based military unit.**

Checks the computer player's soldier count. A soldier is a land-based military unit. Monks and siege weapons are included. [attack-soldier-count](/commands/commands-details.html#attack-soldier-count) + [defend-soldier-count](/commands/commands-details.html#defend-soldier-count) should equal soldier-count.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If the AI has at least 30 soldiers and is in the imperial age, attack.*
```
(defrule
	(soldier-counte >= 30)
	(current-age == imperial-age)
=>
	(attack-now)
	(disable-self)
)
```

相关命令：`attack-soldier-count`, `attack-warboat-count`, `defend-soldier-count`, `defend-warboat-count`, `military-population`, `warboat-count`

### `stance-toward`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Diplomacy, Own Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks if the computer player's diplomatic stance toward a given player matches the given stance.**

Checks if the computer player's diplomatic stance toward a given player matches the given stance, either ally, neutral, or enemy.

To check another player's diplomatic stance toward the computer player, use [players-stance](/commands/commands-details.html#players-stance).

The fact allows "focus-player", "target-player", and "any"/"every" wildcard parameters for [PlayerNumber](/parameters/parameters-details.html#PlayerNumber). It also allows for scenario-player-# and lobby-player-#, where # is between 1 and 8. scenario-player-# refers to the player color (where red = scenario-player-2), and lobby-player-# refers to the player slot (where the lobby host or human player playing a single player campaign is always lobby-player-1).

**参数**：`PlayerNumber` — `PlayerStance`

*示例：Check if we are allied with the focus player.*
```
(defrule
	(stance-toward focus-player ally)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`players-stance`, `set-stance`

### `starting-age`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Game Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the game's starting age.**

Checks the game's starting age. In addition to the regular age parameters, post-imperial-age can be used.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Age`

*示例：Check if the game had a post-imperial start.*
```
(defrule
	(starting-age == post-imperial)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`current-age`, `players-current-age`, `starting-resources`

### `starting-resources`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Game Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks the starting resources level.**

Checks the starting resources level. The standard setting is Low resources. In games without a Starting Resources option, like Death Match, starting-resources will be equal to 1 (low resources), probably because 1 is the standard resource setting in random map games. DE added the option for Ultra High, Infinite, and Random resource starts.

Before DE, AIs on hardest difficulty would get 500 of each resource at the beginning of each age, including at the beginning of the game, but DE no longer does this.

Starting resources can be modified by [sn-add-starting-resource-wood](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-add-starting-resource-wood), [sn-add-starting-resource-food](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-add-starting-resource-food), [sn-add-starting-resource-gold](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-add-starting-resource-gold), or [sn-add-starting-resource-stone](/strategic-numbers/sn-details.html#sn-add-starting-resource-stone), though using these strategic numbers is considered cheating in AI tournaments.

Starting resource amounts:Low Resources: start with 200W, 200F, 100G, and 200S.Medium Resources: start with 500W, 500F, 300G, and 400S.High Resources: start with 1000W, 1000F, 700G, and 800S.Ultra High Resources (DE only): start with 20,000W, 20,000F, 10,000G, and 5000S (same as Death Match).Infinite Resources (DE only): infinite amounts of each resource.Random Resources (DE only): start with random amounts of each resource.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `StartingResources`

*示例：Check if the players started with high resources.*
```
(defrule
	(starting-resources == high)
=>
	(do-nothing)
)
```

相关命令：`cc-add-resource`, `death-match-game`, `food-amount`, `game-type`, `gold-amount`, `starting-age`, `stone-amount`, `wood-amount`, `up-allied-resource-amount`, `up-cc-add-resource`, `up-resource-amount`

相关 SN：`sn-add-starting-resource-wood`, `sn-add-starting-resource-food`, `sn-add-starting-resource-gold`, `sn-add-starting-resource-stone`

### `stone-amount`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Economy · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks a computer player's stone amount.**

Checks a computer player's stone amount. This amount includes escrowed stone.

**参数**：`compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If we have less than 100 stone and less than 3 town centers, set sn-stone-gatherer-percentage to at least 5%.*
```
(defrule
	(stone-amount < 100)
	(building-type-count-total town-center < 3)
=>
	(up-modify-sn sn-stone-gatherer-percentage c:max 5)
)
```

相关命令：`food-amount`, `gold-amount`, `wood-amount`, `up-allied-resource-amount`, `up-resource-amount`

### `strategic-number`
*版本 AoC · 分类 SNs · 复杂度 Low*

**Checks a strategic number's value.**

Checks a strategic number's value.

Strategic numbers modify various built-in behaviors and settings that can modify the automatic behaviors of your AI. See the [SN Index](/strategic-numbers/sn-index.html) for details on what each strategic number does. Each strategic number has a different default value, which you can also check on the SN Index page.

Each SN is given an ID between 0 and 511. Currently, the SNs in the 313-511 range don't appear in the SN index and don't modify the behavior of your AI, but they are available for your AI to use. So, you can modify these SNs however you like, similar to goals, without changing the behavior of your AI. However, if you want to use a strategic number in this way like an extra custom goal, always check the SN index to make sure that the SN ID you are using is actually currently unused. A good practice is to start with using SN 510 (SN 511 might have some bugs in DE) and work your way backwards toward SNs in the 300 range.

**参数**：`SnId` — `compareOp` — `Value`

*示例：If sn-maximum-town-size is less than 24 and the AI is in the Castle Age or the Imperial Age, increase sn-maximum-town-size to 24.*
```
(defrule
	(strategic-number sn-maximum-town-size < 24)
=>
	(set-strategic-number sn-maximum-town-size 24)
)
```

相关命令：`set-strategic-number`, `up-allied-sn`, `up-compare-sn`, `up-modify-sn`

### `taunt-detected`
*版本 AoC · 分类 Chat, Debugging, Other Player Info · 复杂度 Low*

**Detects a given taunt from the given player.**

Detects a given taunt from the given player. The check can be performed any number of times until the taunt is explicitly acknowledged, meaning that if the given taunt is received from the given player, this fact with remain true until the AI uses the [acknowledge-taunt](/commands/commands-details.html#acknowledge-taunt) command to acknowledge the taunt from that player. taunt-detected will detect taunts sent to the AI from another AI that uses the [taunt](/commands/commands-details.html#taunt) command, and it will also detect taunts sent in a chat message if the message starts with a number between 1 and 255.
